# Prism ledger — bugs and work

Two sections. **WORK** tracks the Scene Model work order (five agents, five
worktrees); **BUGS** is the running bug list. New-model bugs carry the tag
`SCENE-MODEL`. Every row is edited in place by its owner; nothing is
deleted — a finished row says `done` and names the merge commit.
Bug ids are allocated per agent to avoid merge collisions: B-1x SM-1 · B-2x SM-4 · B-3x SM-5 · B-4x SM-2 · B-5x SM-3 · B-6x SM-6/orchestrator.

Vocabulary is canonical (CLAUDE.md): App · Slot · Layout · Facet · Scene ·
Canvas class · Visualization · **Frame = the physical device only.**

## WORK — Scene Model era

Charter: the work order of 2026-09-01 (this file's first WORK commit).
Specs, in reading order: `docs/scene-model-spec.md` (all) →
`docs/dashboard-schema.md` §26 / §31 / §32 → `docs/win-host-spec.md` §5 / §3.

Integration order: **SM-1 lands first**; SM-2/3/4 build against its types;
**SM-5 gates every merge**. Renames land as one atomic commit (model + UI +
docs). `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Prism` is backed up before the migration merge.
Stage gate: the Widevine-audio POC (`docs/reports/music-webview2-poc.md`)
must conclude per service (EMBED / HAND-OFF / FAILED-BOTH) before SM-2/SM-4
music work starts.

work order; its *Decision:* lines are where a correction lands). Specs, in
reading order: `docs/scene-model-spec.md` → `docs/dashboard-schema.md` §24 /
§25 / §26 / §27 / §31 → the charter. Bug ids: B-7x CS-1 · B-8x CS-2 ·
B-9x CS-3 · B-10x CS-4 · B-11x CS-5.

CS-4's and CS-5's input); CS-2 and CS-3 are disjoint and run alongside it. The
SM-5 gate applies unchanged: `node scripts/verify.mjs --no-known-gaps
--require-all`, vocabulary audit strict.

**CS-10 dispatch state (orchestrator, 2026-09-03) — CLOSED.**
CS-10 was ordered as six parallel worktree branches. **Nine subagent launches
were killed by a sustained API `529 Overloaded`**, every one before its first
file edit, so their worktrees were auto-cleaned and no partial work was ever
left behind. After the ninth the maintainer stopped dispatching and the rows
were taken **serially in-session instead**, one commit and one gate per row,
each pushed before the next began so an outage would cost one row rather than
five. All six branches exist, gated PASS 15/0/0, and are merged to main:
`cs10-sports` · `cs10-mock4` · `cs10-gallery` · `cs10-agenda` · `cs10-parkers`
· `cs10-mockups`. B-95 landed with CS-10.1.

**The one thing still open in this order: CS-10.2's §14 split-listening chips.**
Its blueprint half landed (hero-plus-two, the derived classes, the `game4`
decision); the chips — rendering `PrivateListening` / `ListenerInfo`, which
exist in core and are still unrendered — were never started, and no host file
was touched for them. Mock 3 already draws them (`Alex ▸ Game 1`,
`Sam ▸ Game 2`, bottom-left), so the mockup is the reference.

Two decisions from the dispatch that outlived it, in case either is revisited:
`DEMO_TRACK.line`'s P4 split belongs to the seed (CS-10.5 shipped it), and the
renderer reads metadata **fields** rather than parsing a joined line, which is
what lets CS-10.1 and CS-10.5 agree without being merged together.

Known gap, deliberately not blocking: Merge ICS subscriptions (work calendars)
are named in Kitchen Command's blurb and left to the Merge roadmap.

**CS-2 as landed** (charter §3, with the deviations named here so a correction
has one place to land):

  Church, Cole, Inness, Gifford, Kensett, Heade, Cropsey, Durand), `packs/cosmos`
  12 (NASA Image and Video Library). Fetched live 2026-09-02; every field is
  from the API response, none invented.
- `pack.json` adds two optional fields to the charter shape: `requiresCreator`
  (pack-level — Gallery declares it, so a painting without a painter FAILS;
  Cosmos does not) and `creditLine` (per image — the institution's own donor
  credit, kept as provenance, never the displayed line).
- The gate returns **PASS**, not NOT-YET, when manifests are valid and the bytes
  are absent: bytes are git-ignored, so a fresh clone must gate green under
  `--require-all`, where NOT-YET counts as FAIL. NOT-YET is reserved for a pack
  with neither entries nor bytes. Absent bytes are reported on every run.
- Host: `ArtService.Pick(source)` reads `Assets/packs/<id>/pack.json` and returns
  image + attribution together; the pool path (`RandomArt`) is unchanged and is
  the fallback, and no art at all still lands on the dark substrate. The §26 card
  gained the attribution line, a "Minimize" toggle and the minimal-mode corner
  chip (credit alone); the §6a context sheet shows the line + `sourceUrl` while a
  veil is up, as an info row — no new route, the five §6a entries are untouched.
- Build: `scripts/stage-packs.mjs` copies the `bundled.txt` subset into
  `Assets/packs` and rewrites the staged manifest to exactly what it copied.
- `scripts/fetch-cosmos-pack.mjs` deleted (superseded); the parked Android
  shell's `assets/packs/cosmos` is left in place with a SUPERSEDED note.
- Not done: §27 gallery-veil slots still draw from the signed pool
  (`prism-art.local`), not from packs — §27 marks slots with a glyph rather than
  a caption, so no attribution line was needed there yet. `veil.ts` untouched.

**Gate step added 2026-09-03 — tile-key allowlist.** `scripts/verify-tile-keys.mjs`
(step 8 of 16). CS-10.3 found that a `tiles:` key can exist in core's
`TILES_KEYS` and in the page, and be missing from the host's `TilesBridge.Keys`
allowlist, with **nothing failing anywhere**: the page loads, the bridge
silently refuses the read, and the facet shows its empty state until a human
looks at the wall. The step fails the BUILD instead. It checks that core's
declared keys and the host's allowlist are the same set, that core actually
ROUTES each key (`readTilesDoc` / `applyTilesIntent`, not merely lists it),
that no page references a key neither side knows, and that every
`https://tiles.prism/<x>/` catalog entry has a page and every page has a
catalog entry. Verified by simulation before wiring: removing `AgendaKey` from
the allowlist makes it FAIL with the right diagnosis, and the host fixtures
CS-10.3 added fail alongside it.

**Who owns a click on the wall (the pattern behind B-98..B-100).** Three bugs in
one evening had the same shape: *something full-screen sitting above the tile
canvas, hit-testable, eating clicks meant for a surface*. The tile canvas — which
carries every surface, including App setup's preview at surface-z 900 — is a
`RootGrid` child at **default z 0**. Everything else the host draws is a sibling
above it: visualization chrome at z 20, listening chips 925, needs-attention
badges 930, the corner affordance 940, the rail 900, the setup card 998. Any of
those that is full-bleed and hit-testable takes the click, and the page below
looks broken rather than covered.

Two rules, learned the hard way:

- **A screen that puts a live surface on the canvas must close whatever else is
  covering it** — at the source, not by dimming the thing on top. `OpenAppSetup`
  and `OpenFacetEditor` call `CloseRail()` for exactly this.
- **Chrome steps aside only for a reveal, never for "some screen is open".** The
  first fix for B-100 also hid the chrome whenever any editor field was non-null;
  one stale field then hid the transport and the tap target for good, and the wall
  had no controls at all. Narrow beats clever.

A host test cannot see z-order, so `host.log` carries the evidence instead: chrome
construction and every visibility change are logged with the ids that decided them.

### How to run the gate (SM-5 merge checklist)

From the repo root of the branch under review, after `npm ci`:
`node scripts/verify.mjs` (≈40 s with the host; `--skip-host` for core-only).
It runs, in order: core `tsc --noEmit` → `vitest run` → dist build →
runtime bundle (`dist/prism-runtime.js`, which the host build copies) →
win-channel reproducibility (regenerate `Channel.g.cs`, `git diff --exit-code`)
→ surface coverage (`scripts/verify-surface-coverage.mjs`, win-host-spec §5)
→ vocabulary audit (`scripts/audit-vocabulary.mjs --strict`; "whitelist" in a
UI string always fails; since the SM-6 rename "frame"-as-slot FAILS by default
— `--lenient-vocab` downgrades it to a report for local runs only; "view", the
pre-model word for facet, is report-only and never fails)
→ §26 presentation fixtures → §6a walk-test → §31 news-shelf reproducibility →
imagery packs (`scripts/verify-imagery-packs.mjs`, concept-scenes §3.3) →
§7 migration round-trip on a COPY → `dotnet test PrismHost.Tests` →
`dotnet build PrismHost -p:Platform=x64` ("Build succeeded", no
`error (CS|MSB|WMC)`). Each sub-check exits 0 PASS / 1 FAIL / 2 NOT-YET (its
input is absent — reported, not failing, until `--require-all`, the post-SM-6
posture: run the gate as `node scripts/verify.mjs --no-known-gaps --require-all`).
A merge needs: **GATE: PASS**, zero NOT-YET rows the branch was
supposed to fill (SM-1: migration + presentation-keeper + news shelf; SM-3:
the `prism://` route registry at `packages/core/routes.registry.json`),
`--no-known-gaps` green on the coverage test for the SM-4 branch, and the
human plans run where they apply (docs/bugs/GOLDEN-PATH.md, MUSIC-TESTS.md,
PRESENTATION-ESC-TEST.md). To gate a branch without merging it, archive its
`packages/core` into a scratch dir, build it there, and pass `--core=` /
`--module=` to the sub-scripts (that is how the SM-1 previews above were run).

| B-269 | CORE | "Some of those top line items have bad dates" (2026-09-22 01:45; the Newest row: The Decameron 1971, What We Do in the Shadows 2014, The Naked Gun 1988-00-00) | Three faults. (1) The lens resolver matched a title's English Wikipedia article by the plain name first, which is the older namesake - the 1971 film for Netflix's 2024 series, the 2014 film for Hulu's series, the 1988 film for the 2025 remake. Fixed: the facts are keyed by title AND kind (a film and a series of one name are two works), the card's kind orders the disambiguations ('(TV series)' before the plain name for a series), and under a key TMDB's word on the work (its kind and year, read first) puts '(2025 film)' first and passes over a plain-name page that names another year; the Wikidata and pageview caches are keyed by the item and article they belong to. (2) A year-only Wikidata date printed as 1988-00-00: shown as its year. (3) FOUND ON THE WAY: the resolver had fired some three hundred summary reads in a second (three slots, five names a title) and Wikipedia answered 429 Too Many Requests - each refusal was then remembered as 'no article' for SEVEN DAYS, which is why most titles had no lens numbers and why a boot 'stalled' on a cache of misses. Fixed: the open sources are read one after another some 350 ms apart with a 15 s rest after a 429, a refusal or a timeout is never remembered, a miss is believed for an hour only, every read has a 25 s timeout so a hung one frees its slot, the host logs a fetch failure (the status, never the key), and PrismRuntime.lensDiag() says what the resolver is doing. LIVE 06:55: The Naked Gun 2025-07-31, 3 From Hell '2019', a cold cache draining at about a title every five seconds (Wikipedia still sends some 429s at that pace; the rest handles them). OPEN: a service that names no kind (Hulu's and Netflix's library rows say 'title') leaves a same-name pair to TMDB's popularity - Better Things read as the 2008 film | main | fixed |

| VS-41 | Browse by genre ("We need to come up with a way to let people browse by genre, need to use only transparent algorithms though, that's the rule", 2026-09-22; design agreed, "Yes proceed") | core + host + docs | done 2026-09-22 - a third tab, Browse: a chip per genre (TMDB's genre list; a chip covers TMDB's movie AND series ids for it, OR'd - e.g. Sci-Fi & Fantasy = 878|14 and 10765), then four rows, each ONE visible number or date from a named source with its formula, source, region and data date on its head and the three-layer disclosure on hover: Yours (the household's titles - Continue watching, My list, the Library - that TMDB files under the genre, A to Z), Top rated on your services (TMDB /discover vote_average.desc, at least 200 votes, an equal mean by more votes), Newest on your services (primary_release_date / first_air_date desc, dated on or before today, at least 10 votes), Most voted on your services (vote_count.desc). 'On your services' is JustWatch's US listing through TMDB matched to the provider ids the signed-in services' adapters claim (the same ids search uses); any offer counts and the card says which (Subscription / Free / Rent / Buy / Owned); a title discover lists but whose own providers name none of the household's services is dropped, not guessed. Never TMDB's popularity sort (a blend TMDB does not fully publish), trending or 'for you' - the fixture asserts no query sorts by popularity. A genre with nothing on the services is a disabled chip with the reason in its tooltip (a chip's count, TMDB's total_results, films + series, read once a day in the background). A card on one service plays on press through the catalog press (the service's own search resolves the exact title - `playCatalog`, shared with search); on several, the press offers the choice. Reads: 6 discover calls per genre + the titles' watch providers (a day cached, keyed by the household's providers), under the household's key. Without a key only Yours shows. Core: `browse.ts` (pure; fixture `browse.test.ts`), orchestrator `videoBrowseStart/Counts/Yours/Card`, runtime `videoBrowse(genre)` / `videoBrowsePlay(app, cardId)`; host: the Browse tab, `BrowseRow`, `FollowBrowseAsync` (one redraw when the rows are in). LIVE 12:52 (dev shot, hub.request 'browse scifi'): Yours 702, Top rated led by Batman: Knightfall Part 1 9.2 (363 votes, Fandango rent/buy) and Avatar: The Last Airbender 9.2 (1.2k votes, Paramount+ subscription +1), Newest led by LEGO Star Wars: The Mandalorian 2026-09-02 (Disney+), Most voted led by Interstellar 41k (Owned on Fandango at Home); counts e.g. Sci-Fi & Fantasy 10,643, Drama 25,492. OFFER FILTER added 13:25 ("Proceed yes"): Fandango at Home rents or sells almost everything, so 'on your services' was close to 'anywhere'. Show: **Included** (the default - with a subscription, free, free with ads, or owned: an owned title plays without paying) / **Anything** (rent and buy too), kept on the device (`video:browse-offer`), printed in every discover row's formula ('US, included with a subscription, free or owned'). Included asks TMDB with `with_watch_monetization_types=flatrate|free|ads`; since TMDB applies that to the title (any provider) rather than to the household's provider, each card then keeps only the household services whose own offer is included (or owned) and a card with none left goes (`offerCounts`, fixture). The genre counts are per filter. LIVE 13:24 (dev shot): Sci-Fi & Fantasy Top rated opens with Avatar: The Last Airbender (Paramount+ subscription) instead of a Fandango rental; Interstellar / Inception stay as Owned; counts 9,653 included vs 10,643 anything. PAGED 13:40 ("Let's fix that known gap"): a row now fills itself - page one of each kind, the merged order, the cards in that order; when matching the household's own services leaves it short (TMDB's offer filter passes a title ANY provider streams), the next page is read and the order rebuilt, up to three pages per kind. The titles already read cost nothing the second time (their providers are kept a day) and the cards are built only as deep as the row needs (BROWSE_LOOKAHEAD). LIVE 13:38: Sci-Fi & Fantasy, Horror and Western all 20 / 20 / 20 under Included (was 17 / 17 / 19). A row still shows fewer only when the genre itself has no more on the services - that count is on its head. NO PAGE FLASH 14:55 ("When I hit browse, there are several reloads where the whole watch page disappears and comes back"): Browse draws into a panel of its own - a genre or offer chip press, and the rows arriving, redraw THAT panel alone (built off-screen, then swapped in); the page itself is torn down only when the tab changes. Before, each chip press and the read finishing called ShowVideoHubAsync, which closes and rebuilds the whole page. READ AHEAD 15:20 ("I pressed browse, Watch page disappeared for a moment and I saw Netflix, and then it came back, still took a moment to fully populate. Is this something that could've already been prepared in the background?"): the page had been held OFF the canvas while Browse was read - the tab's draw was awaited in the middle of the page's build, so the wall showed the service beneath for as long as the read took. Now the page goes up first and the rows land in the tab's panel when they come; and while the person is on Watch or Library the genre they last chose is read ahead once (WarmBrowseAsync, at most every five minutes; core keeps a genre's reads a day), so the press finds it done. LIVE 15:19: the tab switch is 11 ms between 'video hub: closed' and 'open' (was ~240 ms), the rows already full on the first draw. The focus takes the first card when the panel first fills, since the page is now up before the rows exist. IN FULL 15:50 ("I should be able to click one of the lenses like Yours, Top Rated, Newest, and see other items ... 'Yours' has 988 in Action & Adventure but I can't actually see those 988"): a row's head is a button - it opens that row as a GRID, the same rule and the same head (formula, source, region, data date), Back to the rows. Yours is every household title in the genre (989 in Action & Adventure), a catalog row is read deeper - up to BROWSE_FULL_SIZE 300 over BROWSE_FULL_PAGES 12 pages per kind (`videoBrowseFull`, its own day-long cache per genre + offer + size). Both are paged by Show more, sixty at a time, so a thousand posters are never built at once; the press guard knows a full row's cards too. Core: `videoBrowseRow(genre, row, offer, want)`; host: `DrawBrowseFullAsync` / `FollowBrowseFullAsync`, `MenuCardButton` / `BrowseCardButton` / `CardGrid` (one card builder for a row and for a grid). The rows page and the full view both follow while TMDB is still answering for the household's genres ('Yours' grew from 6 to 989 in place after a restart). Dev: hub.request 'browse <genre> <row>'. Nothing is decided now until the toggle's state has stood 3 s; a press is repeated while the page ignores it; done only once the new state has held 3 s; and it acts only on the title's own page. The safety net (core): VideoController keeps each removal from the wall a day (persisted); a list read that still carries it holds it off the My List row and core asks the service again (twice at most, listRetries on record); after that, or the day, it is the household's to keep. | B-308 | Fandango's library read 973 of its 1,917 titles (found 2026-09-24 overnight, measuring the "improve the efficiency and impact of library loads from the fandango and MA services" work) | adapters + core | fixed (fandango 0.2.10) - Fandango's My Movies grid draws ONE 75-title batch at a time, fetched fresh each time (~2.2 s) and placed above the viewport whatever the page's height; the walk moved on as soon as the drawn set changed, and a leftover part of the old batch (21 titles for ~2 s) counted as a change, so whole batches were skipped - and the read was still marked complete, so core dropped titles absent from two reads. Now each step is 85% of the drawn batch's height and waits for titles it had not drawn before (6 s at most); at the end it waits 3 s for the list to grow; a walk that finds no grid yet restarts (it had stopped for good if the grid was late). Measured on the real library, reading only: 1,917 in ~93 s (was 973 in 60 s), My TV 61 in 15 s, Movies Anywhere 1,337 in 53 s (unchanged). A tall hidden page was measured and dropped: no gain on Fandango (one batch whatever the height), and Movies Anywhere's infinite list stops loading on it (60 titles). OWNED_STAY_MS 100 -> 120 s. The Library now holds 2,107 titles (1,190 on both services). Then (2026-09-24, "Just read the list through code" - the page-only rule is out for video by the person's word): the grid's itemIds holds 1,917 slots but only the drawn batch's ids; the batch's signed-in api.vudu.com/api2 request was not visible to XHR / fetch / WebSocket hooks in the page or its blank frames (the person ran the probes; the permission classifier refused them to the assistant) - no direct read, the walk stays. Found on the way: apicache.vudu.com contentSearch answers many contentId segments in one request, and art is by id (poster2/<id>, background/<id>-1280). A 2020 gist's route (the session key lifted from localStorage, purchase history not the library) was not taken. |

Neither Chromium's occlusion switch nor a move or a one-pixel resize brought it back. Fixed: hiding each window's view for one tick and showing it again re-links its picture (tried one method per window on the wall: the hide came back, a re-parent did not) - done for every window when Watch closes and when the wall is restored from a minimize (B-289); nothing reloads, the videos play on. Dev: eval ids "tiles" (the surfaces' XAML state), "nudge", "revive". Verified by the person 2026-09-23. Follow-up the same day ("when I go to watch and come back, peacocktv gets its chrome back temporarily. And this time I also lost windows 3 & 4 again"): the re-link ran 18 ms after Watch closed, while Watch was still composed - luck decided it; and it re-linked the big window too, whose hide/show made Peacock raise its own controls. Now: 400 ms and 1.5 s after Watch closes, the big window (never covered, in Watch's corner) left out; a restore from minimize re-links every window. |

| VS-35 | The best price across the household's services ("It would also be nice if people could see the best price through their available services, in the searches process", 2026-09-22 11:20) | design | NOT BUILT. TMDB's providers data (JustWatch's) says WHERE a title rents or sells and never for how much, and JustWatch's own prices are not in that feed. A price is on each service's own page for the title (Fandango's details page, Prime's, YouTube's, Apple's), so the design is a per-service reader like the search readers: a rent / buy card carries a 'Prices' link; a press asks each signed-in service that carries the title, on its hidden surface, for the title's rent and buy prices (window.__prismVideoPrice(token, {title, id})); the row shows 'Rent $3.99 - Buy $14.99' per service, the best first and marked. Read only on the press (never on every search), cached a day. Needs each service's page read live - Fandango and Movies Anywhere signed in here; YouTube's and Prime's after sign-in | main | open |

(3) Under a key TMDB names the Wikidata item outright (external_ids), and the item names its English article (its sitelink), so no page name is guessed at all - the guessing (five names a title) had been most of the load on Wikipedia; a show's or film's page must read as such in its description (the 1353 Decameron had been taken for the series). | B-271 | WIN-HOST | Found 2026-09-22 09:35: 40,159 of the fresh host.log's 43,230 lines read 'audio session mute failed: Element not found (0x80070490)', twice a second since the PC's restart the day before | The session muter took the default audio endpoint's session manager once and kept the handle; when the default device changed, every pass (a sweep every 1.5 s, each mute ask) failed against the dead handle and said so. Fixed: the handle is dropped on that failure so the next pass re-acquires the endpoint of the moment, and the failure is said once a minute. | B-317 | HOST | **A phone's pairing token reached host.log in the clear on every request** (found 2026-10-04 reading the log for the companion app). B-91's redactor masked `"token":"..."` and `token=` in a query, but the diagnostics tap writes the channel's http call with the inner JSON's quotes as `"`, so the field read `"token":"f74c…"`: no quoted-field match, and the bare-hex rule's word boundary failed between the `2` of the escape and the first hex digit. 3,305 lines in the day's host.log. Fix: the field rule accepts `"` or `"` as the quote (Redact.cs), with a test in that exact shape (RedactTests); the day's log scrubbed in place once (the only time a scrub is right: the write-time rule was wrong). The file never left the device. | host | fixed 2026-10-04 | §22; the token was not rotated (re-pairing the phone); rotate if the log was ever shared |
| B-318 | CORE | **The spot keeper seeks to Prism's saved spot before a service resumes itself** (found 2026-10-05 by the overnight review of the day's commits): runtime.ts spotWatch seeks on the first playing report with pos < 30 when a saved spot is past 60 s, so a service that starts its player at 0 and then jumps to its own, newer resume point (the household watched further on a TV app) is overridden by Prism's older spot; the code's own comment says never when the service resumed itself, but nothing detects that. A product judgment: whose spot wins, Prism's last or the service's? | core | fixed 2026-10-06 | the person's rule: "the position the service thinks you're at should win. Just as a rule." The spot keeper is gone; Prism never seeks to a spot of its own; video:spots stays in the store unread (section 10) |
| B-319 | HOST | **The two routes the host answers itself (POST /remote/diag, GET /audio/device) took any non-empty token** (found 2026-10-05 by the overnight review): every other route is core's, which checks the pairing; these answered an unpaired LAN client, who could fill host.log or read the device and session state. Fix: the host checks the token against core's record of the pairings (remote:tokens) and answers 401 otherwise (511816b3, checked live). Also from that review: the reports server's download address knew stable and beta alone, so an alpha-only release would have sent the website's visitors the older stable zip - fixed in prototypes/prism-veil-reports/server.mjs (alpha known, the plain address sends the newest on any track), **a Fly deploy of prism-reports still to do**. | host | fixed 2026-10-05 (deploy pending) | §19/§22 |

| B-353 | HOST | **YouTube TV's live breaks played uncovered** (2026-10-06, many reports; "Maybe we need to come up with a number of rules ... a confidence interval for ads and veil them if above a certain percentage"). Fix: the break watch (MainWindow.BreakWatch.cs, Services/BreakModel.cs) scores each YouTube TV window's picture every 2 s - the channel's learned logo (pattern match, against the channel's own usual level, kept per channel), cuts to black, what the screen says (Windows' own text reader: web addresses, phone numbers, app lines for an ad; credits and the rating card for the show), the usual lengths - into the chance of a break, sent on as the page's ad-break above 90%; YouTube TV's backstop five minutes (adBackstopMs). | B-354 | CORE | **The playback doctor reloaded a playing Apple TV episode three times and left it on its page** (2026-10-06, "Why did my Apple TV show just stop?" / "It had that much info, it should be able to correct it"). The picture's usual frame rate was the highest ever sampled; a catch-up burst after a restart read above the film's 24, so every plain 24 after it was "behind". The reopens skipped their Play press on the old page's last "playing". Fix: the usual rate is the lowest of the first three samples and rises only on two in a row; a reopen clears the old "playing", goes back to the place it was at, and a title out of tries gets one last open the way a pick plays it. Test: av-sync burst case. | core | fixed 2026-10-06 (0.26.26) | - |

Fix: while the switch is on, each service's background chain also opens its home page on the hidden surface every 4 hours and keeps the shelves alone (keepShelvesFrom); the host names the switch to core (videoSetSuggestions); a page that gives no rows is opened less often (8 h, 16 h, daily); an arrow beside the chip jumps to the rows below Services. Read live after the deploy: Netflix 5 rows and Tubi 5 beside Paramount+'s 2. Still without rows: Peacock (its hidden page stands at the profile gate, and its reader names My Stuff alone), Movies Anywhere (its home page reported no library), Apple TV, Fandango, Prime Video and YouTube (no shelf reader); YouTube TV and Twitch give the person's own rows, drawn always. | core + host + docs | fixed 2026-10-08 for the services whose readers name shelves; the others are adapter work | - |
| B-356 | HOST | **The learned break detector covered a dark film six times in four minutes** (2026-10-08 10:50-10:54, FX's Antlers on the big screen; "not an ad"). Measured on the recorded frames: seven or eight of the last eight frames were black before every one of the six covers, where a cut to black at a break's edge is a frame or two. Fix: BreakModel.DarkScene (four or more black frames in the last eight seconds, no ad words, QR code, known ad picture or marked slot) - the learned detector may not start a cover then unless the rules call the break themselves. | host | fixed 2026-10-08 | - |

| B-358 | TOOLING | **The break watch's kept recordings mixed two dates** (2026-10-08, found when a model trained on two marked days scored worse than on one). The bench names a frame and stamps a line with the time of day alone, and scripts/breakwatch/keep.py filed everything under the date of the keep: the first keep after midnight put the evening before into the new day's folder, and "newer than the last kept line" compared times of day, so each later keep took yesterday's lines again (25 copies of the evening in golden/2026-10-08's logs, the morning before laid over the morning). Fix: keep.py files every frame and line under the date it was recorded on; train2.py reads each day's own lines and frames alone and sets aside the rows whose readings were made from mixed lines. Open: golden/2026-10-08 still holds 2026-10-07's frames and marks (a repair that moves them was written and not run - it needs the person's go-ahead), and the readings for 2026-10-07 after 22:06 need a clean replay. | scripts | keep.py and train2.py fixed 2026-10-08; the folder repair open | - |

The adapter reads the cue ("Cuepoint-Event") from the data the page appends to its buffer, and that is 14 to 31 s before the data plays. So the sure rule (a marked slot covers at once) covered that much of the show wherever a slot opens a break, and stopped that much before the slot's end: on NBC Sports' marked hours 693 of 3,326 slot seconds were the show, and the replay counts 489 s of show covered ahead of a break in six hours (the known-ad library did not learn them: it learns only with an ad's words read and the logo away). Verified live on NBC Sports and NFL Network against the player's own ad mark (starts within 0.1 s). scripts/breakwatch/exams.md has the rows. (2) Hulu's /series/<uuid> address does not mount: it renders Hulu's HOME under the series title, and the play that followed pressed the home hero's tile (Taskmaster S22 E1 started instead of Gilmore Girls, 20:46; that watch is on the account now). Fixed (hulu 0.4.8): a series / movie result carries no address - the press goes through Hulu's own search with the result's id, and the script presses that result once shown (a[data-testid=instant-search-item-link]), which mounts the real page. | B-97 | SCENE-MODEL | **The scene builder threw the FIRST time it was opened in a session** (found on the wall 2026-09-03, clicking + then "add a facet" -> `prism://item/hero/swap`): `System.ArgumentException: Value does not fall within the expected range` from `set_FontSize` in `RenderBuilderPreview()` (MainWindow.SceneBuilder.cs:507). A WinUI element's Width/Height are `double.NaN` until laid out, and `OpenSceneBuilder` renders the preview once synchronously BEFORE the first `SizeChanged` assigns them. Two faults compounded: the guard `if (W <= 0 || H <= 0) return;` does not stop NaN (every comparison with NaN is false), and `Math.Max(11, Math.Min(22, NaN))` is NaN because Math.Min/Max PROPAGATE NaN instead of clamping. So NaN reached `TextBlock.FontSize`. A second open worked, because the overlay is reused with sizes already set - which is why it looked intermittent | orchestrator | **done** - `PrismHost.Core/PreviewGeometry.cs`: `IsRenderable` (finite AND positive - asks whether the size IS usable, never whether it is not bad), `ClampFont(drawn, min, max)` (NaN -> the floor), `LabelFontSize`, `Extent`. Both preview renderers now guard and clamp through it. **The layout editor had the identical latent crash** (`Math.Max(12, Math.Min(28, h / 5))` behind the same NaN-permeable guard) and is fixed in the same commit; each keeps its OWN clamp range (11-22 builder, 12-28 editor) so neither preview changes size. 11 fixtures in `PreviewGeometryTests`, including the old expression asserted to still be NaN so the trap is documented rather than just removed | pre-existing SM-3 code, not introduced by CS-10; the missing `using PrismHost.Layouts` was briefly masked by the known WMC9999 XAML-compiler noise in the same build output |

| B-201 | SCENE-MODEL | "There are 1-3 seconds at the beginning where both Pandora and Spotify ads do make it through. Is there anything we can do to tighten / eradicate that ad time?" (2026-09-15) | core+adapters+spec | done - measured in host.log: Spotify's ad was audible 2.1-3.1 s (the page's Advertisement title at 08:44:03.1, the adapter's report after its own 1 s sustain at :04.23, core's full 1 s window - Spotify declared no sustain - and the mute at :05.24), Pandora's 1.0-1.25 s (500 ms sustain on a 250 ms tick, core's 500 ms remainder). Three changes: (1) mute fast, cover slow - core mutes a mute-audio tile on the FIRST sign and lifts it at once if the signal drops inside the window, the scenery and the soundscape still wait the window (§26 Decision 2026-09-15); (2) both adapters report the first sighting and the first drop with no page-side sustain (Pandora's adSignalSustainedMs is gone); (3) both adapters run their tick from a MutationObserver on the document (40 ms coalesce) with the 250 ms timer standing in. The mute now trails the page's first sign by the observer's coalesce plus the channel - well under a quarter second - and the cover lands at one second as before | |

Now a shuffle bag: every recording once, in a random order, before any repeats, one bag for the wall, kept in the store (soundscape:bag) so a restart carries on, and a new bag never opens with the one just heard. The Random tooltip says so | |

The one capability this PC lacks in Edge and the wall alike is HEVC (MediaSource hvc1/hev1 false, PlayReady + HEVC refused): Paramount+'s and Netflix's HD/4K ladders on Edge are HEVC, so a player without HEVC has no reason to pick PlayReady. Its player asks for nothing better because the SERVER decided at page render: the episode page's own HTML carries player.drm { name: Widevine, widevine: {licence}, playready: {} } - the same in Edge on this PC (read in the connected browser, signed in). | B-263 | CORE | Found 2026-09-21 18:00 walking Paramount+ on the stage: the wall's Forward 10 s moved the stream DURING an ad break, and the page's reader said ad:false throughout | Paramount+ stitches its breaks into the title's own stream (one media element, its length grown by the break) and names them itself: .ad-info-manager with class show ('Advertisement' + the break's counter, top left; class remove in content). Its reader hardcoded ad:false, and the shared seek sets the element's currentTime - so a seek during the break is a skip past the ad, which Prism never does (section 26: observe and cover, never interfere). Fixed in two places: paramountplus 0.3.10 reads the marker (ad:true while it shows) and its seek does nothing in a break (answering handled, so core's element fallback does not seek either); and core refuses seekforward / seekbackward on ANY tile whose page reports an ad (VideoCore.inAd; the verb answers unavailable; tests/video.test.ts). A station played from now on is remembered and restarted by id | the point stored before this (home + "Born This Way") holds no station and cannot be restarted; the next station played is |

| B-152 | SCENE-MODEL | "the visualizations in Prism are picking up the audio from my conference call in a different app ... I would've expected it to follow the music, even if muted" (2026-09-08) | host | done (first half) - the FFT taps a PROCESS-scoped loopback (Windows 10 2004+ VAD\\Process_Loopback) on the stage source's own WebView2 browser process, re-checked every 3 s (a recycle, a late source); the device loopback stands in where activation fails. Measured: a source muted through WebView2 renders zeros, so the loopback cannot follow it. Second half: hidden music sources are muted at their Windows AUDIO SESSION (ISimpleAudioVolume over the browser process tree, re-applied at session creation and by a 1 s sweep) - the engine mutes at the mix, after the process rendered, so the loopback still hears the music; the WebView2 mute stands in when no session exists yet. The soundscape itself: Chromium's autoplay policy kept the page's AudioContext suspended - it never had a click - and every time it "worked" an injected probe had resumed it; now its own profile runs with the policy relaxed and core injects the play call at load. Windows remembers a session's volume per app, so a session born after an unmute came up at the old duck ("unmuted the button but no music until I muted and unmuted again") - new sessions and the sweep now apply the wanted state in both directions. The wall froze once (the UI thread waited on the muter's lock while its sweep thread sat in a COM call that needed the UI thread): every session call now runs on one MTA worker and the UI thread only enqueues; the WebView2 mute holds until a session has actually been ducked | |
